+// ── the WALL detector (pure — no Chrome needed, so it runs before the skip below) ──────────────────────
+{
+  // ── [W] THE WALL: shape detection of UNKNOWN values (PII-PLUGIN-SPEC §9) ──────────────────────────────
+    // The pii plugin's store-keyed masking cannot catch a value nobody registered. detectWallShapes finds ones that merely LOOK
+    // like an identifier. Precision matters more than recall here: the wall's main ingress is browser
+    // network/console output, so a rule that fires on every log timestamp would get rubber-stamped in a day.
+    {
+      console.log('\n[W] wall — unknown-PII shape detection');
+      const { detectWallShapes } = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+      const found = (t, r = []) => detectWallShapes(t, r).map((h) => h.label + ':' + h.value);
+      const one = (t) => found(t)[0] || '(none)';
+
+      ok(one('id 123456789 here') === 'SSN:123456789', 'W1: a bare 9-digit run is an SSN');
+      ok(one('id 123-45-6789 here') === 'SSN:123-45-6789', 'W2: the 3-2-4 hyphen layout matches');
+      ok(one('id 123 45 6789 here') === 'SSN:123 45 6789', 'W3: the 3-2-4 space layout matches');
+      ok(one('1002003004') === 'EDI:1002003004', 'W4: a bare 10-digit run is an EDI');
+      // The developer's boundary rule: bounded by NON-numbers, so glued to a letter still counts...
+      // W5 originally asserted the digit-only boundary (any letter-glued run matched). The developer reversed
+      // that: glue means opaque token, UNLESS the glue is the field's own label. See phase [X2].
+      ok(one('SSN111223333') === 'SSN:111223333', 'W5: a LABEL-glued run still matches (SSN + 9 digits)');
+      ok(found('gh111223333').length === 0, 'W5b: ...but an opaque alphanumeric token does not');
+      // ...but a 9-digit window inside a longer number is NOT an id.
+      ok(found('1234567890123').length === 0, 'W6: a 13-digit run yields nothing (no digit-bounded 9 or 10 inside it)');
+      ok(found('12345678').length === 0, 'W7: 8 digits is not a declared shape');
+
+      // The two false positives that would make the wall unusable on browser output.
+      ok(found('2026-07-25 19:44:51').length === 0, 'W8: a log TIMESTAMP is not an EDI (declared groupings only)');
+      ok(found('192.168.1.100').length === 0, 'W9: an IPv4 is not an EDI (dot is not a group separator)');
+      ok(found('2024-01-15').length === 0, 'W10: an ISO date is not an id');
+      ok(found('v1.2.3 build 1476').length === 0, 'W11: versions/build numbers are not ids');
+
+      ok(one('mail john.doe+x@mail.example.com now') === 'Email:john.doe+x@mail.example.com', 'W12: email matches');
+
+      // ZIP is deliberately BROAD ("err on the side of blocking too much"): 5 digits, so it also catches ports and
+      // other bare 5-digit numbers. Affordable only because the surface is page TEXT, not source.
+      ok(one('Reston, VA 20191') === 'ZIP:20191', 'W16: a 5-digit ZIP matches');
+      ok(one('port 17904') === 'ZIP:17904', 'W17: over-blocking is INTENDED — a bare 5-digit port also fires');
+      ok(found('2026-07-25 19:44:51').length === 0, 'W18: ZIP does not resurrect the timestamp false positive');
+
+      // STREET ADDRESS — house number (<=5 digits) + a word. The noisiest rule by far; the capitalized-word
+      // lever is what keeps it from firing on every numeric label.
+      ok(one('123 Main St') === 'Address:123 Main', 'W19: a house number + street name matches');
+      ok(one('7 Elm') === 'Address:7 Elm', 'W20: a 1-digit house number matches (<=5 digits)');
+      ok(found('3 items').length === 0 && found('5 files deleted').length === 0 && found('404 error').length === 0,
+        'W21: the capital lever drops lowercase numeric labels ("3 items", "404 error")');
+      ok(one('12 Comments') === 'Address:12 Comments', 'W22: a CAPITALIZED numeric label still fires — accepted over-block, not a bug');
+      ok(found('123456 Main').length === 0, 'W23: a 6-digit run is not a house number (digit-bounded)');
+      // Overlap: the address span wins over the bare ZIP inside it.
+      ok(one('22042 Oak Lane') === 'Address:22042 Oak', 'W24: address beats the ZIP inside it (longest-wins)');
+
+      // Never re-flag what the store-keyed masker already tokenized.
+      const tok = 'see @@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn) ok';
+      ok(detectWallShapes(tok, [[4, 4 + '@@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn)'.length]]).length === 0, 'W13: a reserved token span is skipped');
+
+      // Overlap: the grouped form wins over any bare run inside it, and both ids in one line are found.
+      const two = found('a 123-45-6789 b 1002003004 c');
+      ok(two.length === 2 && two[0] === 'SSN:123-45-6789' && two[1] === 'EDI:1002003004', `W14: two ids on one line, longest-wins, in order (${two.join(' | ')})`);
+      const offs = detectWallShapes('xx 123456789 yy');
+      ok(offs[0].start === 3 && offs[0].end === 12, `W15: offsets are exact (${offs[0].start}-${offs[0].end})`);
+    }
+
+}
+
+// ── [X] the WALL: scan surface, session decisions, ephemeral tokens (pure — no Chrome) ────────────────
+{
+  console.log('\n[X] wall — surface, decisions, tokens');
+  const W = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+  W._wallReset();
+  const src = W.wallSourceKey('sess', 'http://host/patient?ssn=482-11-9037');
+
+  // The query can itself carry PII, so it is scanned as CONTENT but never used as a key or a label.
+  ok(src === 'browser:sess@http://host/patient', `X1: the source key drops the query string (${src})`);
+
+  // SCAN SURFACE: class/style/placeholder blanked, everything else kept. `id` is deliberately kept.
+  const html = '<div class="grid-12345" style="width:22042px" id="row-55501"><input value="482-11-9037" placeholder="000-00-0000"></div>';
+  const surf = W.wallScanSurface(html);
+  ok(surf.length === html.length, 'X2: blanking preserves LENGTH, so match offsets still index the original');
+  const labels = W.wallScan(html).map((h) => h.label + ':' + h.value);
+  ok(labels.includes('SSN:482-11-9037'), `X3: PII in an ATTRIBUTE is caught (${labels.join(', ')})`);
+  ok(!labels.some((l) => /12345|22042/.test(l)), 'X4: class + style numbers are NOT scanned');
+  ok(!labels.some((l) => l.includes('000-00-0000')), 'X5: a placeholder format hint is NOT scanned');
+  ok(labels.includes('ZIP:55501'), 'X6: an id IS scanned (recall over quiet, by decision)');
+
+  // Offsets index the ORIGINAL text even though the scan ran on the blanked copy.
+  const text = 'line one\nSSN 482-11-9037 here\nline three\nline four';
+  const hits = W.wallScan(text);
+  ok(text.slice(hits[0].start, hits[0].end) === '482-11-9037', 'X7: an offset from the blanked scan slices the ORIGINAL correctly');
+
+  // FAIL-CLOSED: with no decision recorded the text is returned UNCHANGED and the match is pending.
+  let r = W.wallApply(text, hits, src);
+  ok(r.pending.length === 1 && r.text === text && r.masked === 0, 'X8: an undecided match is PENDING and nothing is released');
+
+  // A mask decision tokenizes; the token is unlike the pii plugin's @@@()/###{} so pii can never try to resolve it.
+  W.wallRemember(src, '482-11-9037', 'mask');
+  r = W.wallApply(text, W.wallScan(text), src);
+  ok(r.masked === 1 && !r.text.includes('482-11-9037'), `X9: a mask decision tokenizes the value (${r.text.split('\n')[1]})`);
+  ok(!/@@@|###/.test(r.text), 'X10: the wall token cannot be mistaken for a pii store token');
+  ok(W.wallResolve(r.text) === text, 'X11: the plugin resolves its OWN token back for type/fill');
+
+  // Normalization: one decision covers both separator spellings.
+  ok(W.wallNormalize('555-012-3456') === W.wallNormalize('555 012 3456'), 'X12: separator spellings fold to ONE decision');
+
+  // An allow decision leaves the value alone and is no longer pending.
+  W._wallReset();
+  W.wallRemember(src, '482-11-9037', 'allow');
+  r = W.wallApply(text, W.wallScan(text), src);
+  ok(r.allowed === 1 && r.pending.length === 0 && r.text === text, 'X13: an allow decision passes the value through');
+
+  // A DIFFERENT source asks again — a value approved on a fixture must not pass out of a real record.
+  const other = W.wallSourceKey('sess', 'http://host/other');
+  r = W.wallApply(text, W.wallScan(text), other);
+  ok(r.pending.length === 1, 'X14: the same value from a DIFFERENT source is pending again');
+  // ...unless it was remembered for all sources.
+  W.wallRemember(src, '482-11-9037', 'allow', true);
+  r = W.wallApply(text, W.wallScan(text), other);
+  ok(r.pending.length === 0, 'X15: "apply to all sources" covers a new source');
+
+  // Multiple matches on one line splice right-to-left, so every offset stays valid.
+  W._wallReset();
+  const two = 'a 482-11-9037 b 1002003004 c';
+  for (const h of W.wallScan(two)) W.wallRemember(src, h.value, 'mask');
+  const r2 = W.wallApply(two, W.wallScan(two), src);
+  ok(r2.masked === 2 && !/482|1002003004/.test(r2.text), `X16: two matches on one line both tokenize (${r2.text})`);
+  ok(W.wallResolve(r2.text) === two, 'X17: both round-trip back');
+
+  const c = W.wallContext(text, hits[0]);
+  ok(c.line === 2 && c.before[0] === 'line one' && c.after[0] === 'line three', `X18: context carries lines above + below (line ${c.line})`);
+  W._wallReset();
+}

+// ── [Z] the WALL under ATTACK — regressions for bugs found by adversarial probing ─────────────────────
+// Every case here FAILED before the fix in the same commit. They are the ways a wall stops being a wall:
+// it re-prompts forever, or it hands back the value it was meant to hide.
+{
+  console.log('\n[Z] wall — adversarial');
+  const W = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+  const { wallScan, wallApply, wallRemember, wallResolve, wallSourceKey, wallToken, _wallReset } = W;
+  const src = W.wallSourceKey('s', 'http://h/p');
+
+  // Z1. THE WALL MUST NOT FLAG WHAT IT JUST MASKED. Token ids were a counter; once it passed 10000 the id
+  // contained five digits and read as a ZIP, so every masked value prompted again on the next scan.
+  _wallReset();
+  wallRemember(src, '482-11-9037', 'mask');
+  const masked = wallApply('SSN 482-11-9037 here', wallScan('SSN 482-11-9037 here'), src);
+  ok(wallScan(masked.text).length === 0, `Z1: re-scanning masked output finds NOTHING (${masked.text})`);
+
+  // Z2. TOKEN IDS MUST BE UNGUESSABLE. Sequential ids meant seeing one token handed you every other one.
+  _wallReset();
+  const t1 = wallToken('111-22-3333');
+  const t2 = wallToken('999-88-7777');
+  ok(!/wall:\d+⟧/.test(t1), `Z2: an id is not a plain counter (${t1})`);
+  ok(t1 !== t2 && t1.length === t2.length && t1.length > 20, 'Z3: ids are wide and distinct');
+
+  // Z4. A FORGED token — one the plugin never issued — must never resolve to a real value. This is the
+  // exfiltration path: the agent reaching a value it was never shown.
+  ok(wallResolve('⟦wall:1⟧') === '⟦wall:1⟧', 'Z4: a made-up short id does not resolve');
+  ok(wallResolve('⟦wall:0000000000000000⟧') === '⟦wall:0000000000000000⟧', 'Z5: a well-formed but unissued id does not resolve');
+
+  // Z6. A HOSTILE PAGE printing token syntax must not have it substituted with PII on the way back in.
+  const spoof = 'the page printed ⟦wall:0123456789abcdef⟧ itself';
+  ok(wallResolve(spoof) === spoof, 'Z6: page-supplied token syntax is inert');
+  // ...while a token the plugin DID issue still round-trips, because that is the whole point of masking.
+  ok(wallResolve(`type ${t1} in`) === 'type 111-22-3333 in', 'Z7: a genuinely issued token still resolves');
+
+  // Z8. Offsets are computed on a BLANKED copy — they must still index the original when the text contains
+  // surrogate pairs (JS length is UTF-16 units on both sides, but only a test proves it).
+  const uni = 'emoji \u{1F600}\u{1F600} then SSN 482-11-9037 end';
+  const uh = wallScan(uni);
+  ok(uh.length === 1 && uni.slice(uh[0].start, uh[0].end) === '482-11-9037', 'Z8: offsets survive surrogate pairs');
+
+  // Z9. Several masks in one string splice right-to-left; the text must stay coherent and round-trip exactly.
+  _wallReset();
+  const line = 'ids 1234567890 and 12345 here';
+  for (const h of wallScan(line)) wallRemember(src, h.value, 'mask');
+  const sp = wallApply(line, wallScan(line), src);
+  ok(sp.masked === 2 && wallResolve(sp.text) === line, `Z9: multi-mask splices cleanly and round-trips (${sp.text})`);
+
+  // Z10. A decision must not cover a DIFFERENT value that merely normalizes nearby.
+  _wallReset();
+  wallRemember(src, '12345', 'allow');
+  ok(wallApply('zip 54321', wallScan('zip 54321'), src).pending.length === 1, 'Z10: a different value is still asked about');
+
+  // Z11. Cost guard: a large result must not stall the tool call behind the scan.
+  const big = 'lorem 12345 ipsum 482-11-9037 dolor\n'.repeat(4000);
+  const t0 = Date.now();
+  const n = wallScan(big).length;
+  const ms = Date.now() - t0;
+  ok(ms < 3000 && n > 0, `Z11: a ~150KB result scans in ${ms}ms (${n} hits)`);
+  _wallReset();
+}
+
+// ── [X2] EXEMPTIONS — noise the developer saw in real output and chose not to be asked about ──────────
+{
+  console.log('\n[X2] wall — exemptions');
+  const W = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+  const flags = (t) => W.wallScan(t).length > 0;
+
+  // A number glued to letters is an identifier/hash/build artefact, not a person. The SPACE is what makes a
+  // number stand alone. NOTE this REVERSES the original digit-only boundary rule.
+  ok(!flags('gh42536'), 'X2-1: a number glued into an alphanumeric token is allowed (gh42536)');
+  ok(flags('gh 42536'), 'X2-2: ...but the same number standing alone still counts (gh 42536)');
+  ok(!flags('2000B'), 'X2-3: a byte size is allowed (2000B) — the same glue rule');
+  ok(!flags('x1002003004'), 'X2-4: glue applies to every id shape, not just short ones');
+
+  // EXCEPT when the glue is the field's own LABEL — that is an identifier announcing itself.
+  ok(flags('SSN482119037'), 'X2-5: "SSN" + 9 digits still counts — the glue is a LABEL, not an opaque token');
+  ok(flags('ssn482119037') && flags('Ssn482119037'), 'X2-6: the label test is case-insensitive');
+  ok(flags('482119037ssn'), 'X2-7: a trailing label counts too');
+  ok(!flags('abc482119037'), 'X2-8: a NON-label prefix is still just an opaque token');
+
+  // An HTTP verb beside a number makes it a status code / size, not a person.
+  ok(!flags('GET 20012'), 'X2-9: a number after an HTTP verb is allowed (GET 20012)');
+  ok(!flags('20012 GET'), 'X2-10: ...and before one (20012 GET) — the address shape SWALLOWS the verb, so the test looks inside the match too');
+  ok(!flags('POST 48211') && !flags('19 GET'), 'X2-11: the verb list generalises beyond GET');
+
+  // None of this blunts the real thing.
+  ok(flags('SSN 482-11-9037') && flags('Reston VA 20191') && flags('1002003004') && flags('a@b.co'),
+    'X2-12: real PII shapes are untouched by the exemptions');
+  ok(flags('123 Main St'), 'X2-13: a street address is untouched');
+
+  // The rule that fired is nameable, so a surprising allow can be explained rather than guessed at.
+  const m = { shape: 'zip', label: 'ZIP', value: '42536', start: 3, end: 8 };
+  ok(W.wallExemption('gh42536', m) === 'alnum-glue', `X2-14: an exemption names itself (${W.wallExemption('gh42536', m)})`);
+}

+if (!CHROME) { console.log('Chrome not installed — skipping browser-debug e2e (not a failure).'); process.exit(0); }

+// Which socket the plugin will use: Node ships a global WebSocket only from v21, so on Node 18/20 phases A-G run

+// A page with: a console.log, a deliberate uncaught error, a button that mutates the DOM + fires two fetches

+  });
+  setTimeout(() => { throw new Error('deliberate boom'); }, 40);
+</script></body></html>`;
+
+async function main() {
+  // ── test server ──
+  const server = createServer((req, res) => {
+    if (req.url === '/api/data') { res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json' }); res.end('{"value":42}'); return; }
+    if (req.url === '/api/missing') { res.writeHead(404); res.end('nope'); return; }
+    res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/html' }); res.end(PAGE);
+  });
+  await new Promise((r) => server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', r));
+  const base = `http://127.0.0.1:${server.address().port}`;
+
+  // ── mock host: spawn launches Chrome (tracked-registry stand-in) ──
+  const children = new Map(); let n = 0;
+  // The wall is ALWAYS ON, so every tool result is scanned and a match blocks the call until decided. The platform
+  // supplies that UI in production; here the mock host stands in for the developer and ALLOWS. Without this the
+  // suite would fail closed — which is the wall working, not a broken test.
+  const wallAsked = [];
+  let wallAnswer = 'keep';    // the developer's REVEAL decision: 'reveal' | 'keep'
+  let preMask = (t) => t;     // stands in for the platform's content filters; phase [P] makes it real
+  const host = {
+    log: () => {},
+    request: async (type, payload) => {
+      // The wall asks via the GENERIC blocking-panel request; the platform carries no PII vocabulary. The tree is
+      // the plugin's, so the test reads the choice ids out of it exactly as the real UI would.
+      // The wall pre-masks through the PLATFORM's content filters before scanning. `preMask` stands in for them:
+      // identity by default, and phase [P] swaps in a real pii-style masker.
+      if (type === 'mask-content') return { text: preMask(String(payload.text || '')) };
+      if (type !== 'ask-panel') throw new Error('unexpected host request: ' + type);
+      // The ONLY prompt now is a reveal request: the agent asked to see one value. wallAnswer stands in for the
+      // developer's decision ('reveal' | 'keep').
+      wallAsked.push(payload);
+      const values = {};
+      const walk = (n) => { if (!n || typeof n !== 'object') return;
+        if (n.type === 'choice') values[n.id] = wallAnswer;
+        for (const c of (n.children || [])) walk(c); };
+      walk(payload.tree);
+      return { submitted: wallAnswer !== 'skip', values: wallAnswer === 'skip' ? null : values };
+    },
+    spawn: (file, args) => { const c = spawn(file, [...args, '--no-sandbox'], { windowsHide: true, stdio: 'ignore' }); const id = 'bg-' + (++n); children.set(id, c); return { id, pid: c.pid }; },
+    killSpawned: (id) => { const c = children.get(id); if (c) { try { if (process.platform === 'win32') spawnSync('taskkill', ['/F', '/T', '/PID', String(c.pid)]); else c.kill(); } catch {} children.delete(id); } return { killed: [id] }; },
+  };
+
+  const mod = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+  const plugin = mod.default;
+  plugin.init(host);
+  const tool = (verb) => { const t = plugin.tools.find((x) => x.verb === verb); return (args) => t.impl(args, {}).then((r) => (r && r.content != null) ? r.content : r).catch((e) => 'THREW: ' + e.message); };
+  const call = {};
+  for (const t of plugin.tools) call[t.verb] = tool(t.verb);
+
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[A] open a headless session + navigate');
+    const opened = await call.browser_open({ label: 'dbg', headless: true, url: base + '/' });
+    ok(/started|attached/.test(opened) && !/THREW/.test(opened), `A1: session opened (${opened.slice(0, 60)})`);
+    if (/THREW/.test(opened)) throw new Error('open failed: ' + opened);
+    await sleep(300);
+    ok(/dbg:/.test(await call.browser_list({})), 'A2: browser_list shows it');
+
+    console.log('\n[B] snapshot gives a text tree with refs; find locates the button');
+    const snap = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+    ok(/Debug Me/.test(snap) && /button/.test(snap) && /\[e\d+\]/.test(snap), `B1: snapshot has title + a button + refs (${snap.split('\n').slice(0, 4).join(' | ').slice(0, 80)})`);
+    const goRef = (snap.match(/\[(e\d+)\][^\n]*"?Go"?/) || snap.match(/\[(e\d+)\] button/))?.[1];
+    ok(!!goRef, `B2: found the Go button's ref (${goRef})`);
+    const inputRef = (snap.match(/\[(e\d+)\][^\n]*your name/) || [])[1] || (snap.match(/\[(e\d+)\] textbox/) || [])[1];
+
+    console.log('\n[C] eval reads live global state; console + errors captured');
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.appState.count' })).trim() === '0', 'C1: eval reads window.appState.count = 0');
+    await sleep(200);   // let the deliberate error fire
+    ok(/page loaded v1/.test(await call.browser_console({ label: 'dbg' })), 'C2: console shows the page log');
+    ok(/deliberate boom/.test(await call.browser_errors({ label: 'dbg' })), 'C3: errors captures the uncaught exception');
+
+    console.log('\n[D] click by ref mutates state + DOM + fires network; timeline records it');
+    await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: goRef });
+    await sleep(400);
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.appState.count' })).trim() === '1', 'D1: the click ran — appState.count is now 1');
+    const net = await call.browser_network({ label: 'dbg' });
+    ok(/\/api\/data/.test(net) && /200/.test(net), 'D2: network shows /api/data 200');
+    const failed = await call.browser_network({ label: 'dbg', failedOnly: true });
+    ok(/\/api\/missing/.test(failed) && /404/.test(failed), 'D3: failedOnly shows /api/missing 404');
+    const tl = await call.browser_timeline({ label: 'dbg' });
+    ok(/DOM:.*\+.*banner/i.test(tl) || /\+div\.banner/i.test(tl), `D4: timeline shows the DOM diff from the click (+div.banner) (${(tl.match(/DOM:[^\n]*/) || [''])[0].slice(0, 70)})`);
+    ok(/NETWORK/i.test(tl) && /CONSOLE/i.test(tl), 'D5: timeline interleaves network + console events');
+
+    console.log('\n[E] request detail, wait, dom, storage, type');
+    const reqId = (failed.match(/^(\S+)\s/m) || [])[1];
+    if (reqId) ok(/status: 404/.test(await call.browser_request({ label: 'dbg', requestId: reqId })), 'E1: browser_request returns the 404 detail');
+    ok(/condition met|clicked 1/.test(await call.browser_wait({ label: 'dbg', text: 'clicked 1', timeoutMs: 3000 })), 'E2: browser_wait finds the injected text');
+    if (inputRef) { await call.browser_type({ label: 'dbg', ref: inputRef, text: 'Alice' }); ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'document.getElementById("name").value' })).includes('Alice'), 'E3: browser_type set the input value'); }
+    ok(/COMPUTED|display/.test(await call.browser_dom({ label: 'dbg', ref: goRef })), 'E4: browser_dom returns computed styles for a ref');
+    await call.browser_storage({ label: 'dbg', setLocal: { token: 'abc123' } });
+    ok(/abc123/.test(await call.browser_storage({ label: 'dbg' })), 'E5: browser_storage set + read localStorage');
+
+    console.log('\n[F] a stale ref is a clear error, not a wrong action');
+    ok(/stale|unknown ref/i.test(await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: 'e9999' })), 'F1: clicking a stale ref errors clearly');
+
+    // el.click() dispatches ONE untrusted `click` and nothing else — no pointerdown/mousedown, no focus,
+    // isTrusted false — so real UIs ignored it while the plugin reported "clicked". Interaction now goes through
+    // CDP Input (trusted events), and the cases where a click CANNOT land are named instead of claimed as success.
+    console.log('\n[I] interaction is REAL input; results are legible');
+    await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: '(window.ev = {}, "cleared")' });
+    const snap2 = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+    const refFor = (name) => { const l = snap2.split('\n').find((x) => x.includes('"' + name + '"')); return l ? (/\[(e\d+)\]/.exec(l) || [])[1] : null; };
+
+    await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: refFor('Mousedown Only') });
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.ev.mousedn || 0' })).trim() === '1', 'I1: a MOUSEDOWN-only handler fires (el.click() never reached it)');
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.ev["mousedn:trusted"]' })).trim() === 'true', 'I2: the events are TRUSTED');
+    await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: refFor('Trusted Only') });
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.ev.trustedonly || 0' })).trim() === '1', 'I3: an isTrusted-gated click handler fires');
+
+    const cov = await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: refFor('Covered Btn') });
+    ok(/painted on top|received the click/i.test(cov), `I4: an overlay-covered element names the BLOCKER, not "clicked" (${cov.slice(0, 60)})`);
+    const dis = await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: refFor('Disabled Btn') });
+    ok(/DISABLED/.test(dis), `I5: a disabled element says so (${dis.slice(0, 55)})`);
+
+    const rrRef = refFor('Rerender Me');
+    await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.rerenderNow()' });
+    const staleRes = await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg', ref: rrRef });
+    ok(/no longer in the page/i.test(staleRes), `I6: a re-rendered node ERRORS instead of clicking the detached orphan (${staleRes.slice(0, 55)})`);
+
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.rerenderNow()' })) === 'undefined', 'I7: a void expression returns "undefined", not an EMPTY result');
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.nothingHere' })) === 'undefined', 'I8: a missing property returns "undefined"');
+    ok(/^<body/.test(await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'document.body' })), 'I9: a DOM node renders as <body…>, not {}');
+    ok(/^\[Function/.test(await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.rerenderNow' })), 'I10: a function renders as [Function …], not {}');
+    ok(/needs a ref/.test(await call.browser_click({ label: 'dbg' })), 'I11: click with neither ref nor selector is a clear error');
+
+    await call.browser_type({ label: 'dbg', ref: refFor('your name'), text: 'Bob' });
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.ev["nameinput:trusted"]' })).trim() === 'true', 'I12: typing fires a TRUSTED input event (controlled React/Vue fields update)');
+    ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'document.getElementById("name").value' })).includes('Bob'), 'I13: the typed value actually landed');
+
+    // ── [Y] AUTO-MASK, then reveal on request ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
+    // The blocking review screen produced 8-20 prompts per real page load, which is how a wall gets clicked
+    // through. Inverted: mask silently, tell the agent, and spend a developer decision only when the AGENT asks
+    // for a specific value.
+    console.log('\n[Y] wall — auto-mask + reveal on request');
+    {
+      const W = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+      W._wallReset();
+      wallAsked.length = 0;
+      const snapY = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+
+      ok(wallAsked.length === 0, `Y1: NOTHING was asked — masking is silent (${wallAsked.length} prompts)`);
+      ok(!/482-11-9037/.test(snapY), 'Y2: the SSN in the page text was masked anyway');
+      ok(/wall:[0-9a-f]{16}/.test(snapY), 'Y3: a token stands in its place');
+      ok(/matched a sensitive-data pattern/.test(snapY), 'Y4: the agent is TOLD masking happened, in the result itself');
+      ok(/reveal_masked/.test(snapY), 'Y5: ...and how to ask for a value it needs');
+      ok(/suspicion, not a finding/.test(snapY), 'Y6: the note does NOT claim a type — a matcher fired, which is not proof of an SSN');
+
+      // The id the agent would quote back.
+      const id = (snapY.match(/wall:([0-9a-f]{16})/) || [])[1];
+      ok(!!id, 'Y7: the token carries a quotable id');
+
+      // DENIED reveal: the value stays masked and the agent is told why.
+      wallAnswer = 'keep';
+      const denied = await call.reveal_masked({ id });
+      ok(wallAsked.length === 1, 'Y8: asking to reveal is what prompts the developer — one value, one decision');
+      ok(/THREW/.test(denied) && /declined/.test(denied), `Y9: a denial keeps it masked and says so (${denied.slice(0, 60)})`);
+
+      // APPROVED reveal: the real value comes back...
+      wallAnswer = 'reveal';
+      const shown = await call.reveal_masked({ id });
+      ok(shown.trim() === '482-11-9037' || shown.includes('482-11-9037'), `Y10: an approved reveal returns the real value (${shown.slice(0, 40)})`);
+      // ...and because approval means "not sensitive", it stops being masked everywhere from now on.
+      const after = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+      ok(/482-11-9037/.test(after), 'Y11: a revealed value is no longer masked — approval means it was a false positive');
+      const asksBefore = wallAsked.length;
+      await call.reveal_masked({ id });
+      ok(wallAsked.length === asksBefore, 'Y12: an already-revealed value does not re-prompt');
+
+      // A forged / unknown id must not resolve, and must say why rather than implying the value never existed.
+      const bogus = await call.reveal_masked({ id: '0'.repeat(16) });
+      ok(/THREW/.test(bogus) && /no masked value/.test(bogus), `Y13: an unissued id is refused (${bogus.slice(0, 50)})`);
+      const malformed = await call.reveal_masked({ id: 'not-an-id' });
+      ok(/THREW/.test(malformed) && /16 hex/.test(malformed), 'Y14: a malformed id gets a clear error');
+
+      wallAnswer = 'keep';
+      W._wallReset();
+    }
+    // ── [E2] the GATE's uncovered paths — errors, label-less verbs, session isolation ─────────────────────
+    // Phase [Y] proves the happy path. These are the ways a result reaches the agent AROUND the gate.
+    console.log('\n[E2] wall — gate coverage');
+    {
+      const W = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+      W._wallReset();
+      wallAnswer = 'mask';
+      wallAsked.length = 0;
+
+      // A tool ERROR is a result too, and browser errors quote page content verbatim. This bypassed the gate
+      // entirely until the error path was wrapped — the message went straight to the agent.
+      const evalErr = await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.wallBoom()' });
+      ok(/THREW/.test(evalErr), 'E2-1: the failing eval still surfaces as an error');
+      ok(!/482-11-9037/.test(evalErr), `E2-2: a page exception carrying an SSN does NOT reach the agent (${evalErr.slice(0, 70)})`);
+      ok(/wall:[0-9a-f]{16}/.test(evalErr), 'E2-3: it is masked, so the error keeps its diagnostic shape');
+
+      // Errors AUTO-MASK rather than prompt: a failure path is the wrong moment for a modal.
+      const asksBefore = wallAsked.length;
+      await call.browser_eval({ label: 'dbg', js: 'window.wallBoom2()' });
+      ok(wallAsked.length === asksBefore, 'E2-4: an error masks WITHOUT prompting');
+
+      // A verb with no `label` still passes the gate (a degenerate source key must not skip scanning).
+      const list = await call.browser_list({});
+      ok(!/482-11-9037/.test(list), 'E2-5: a label-less verb is scanned too');
+
+      // A SECOND session is a different source, so a decision made on the first must not carry over.
+      await call.browser_open({ label: 'dbg2', headless: true, url: base + '/' });
+      const before = wallAsked.length;
+      const snap2 = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg2' });
+      ok(wallAsked.length === before, `E2-6: a second session masks WITHOUT prompting (${before} -> ${wallAsked.length})`);
+      ok(!/482-11-9037/.test(snap2), 'E2-7: and its output is masked');
+      await call.browser_close({ label: 'dbg2' });
+
+      // A value repeated in one result is ONE question. Asking per-occurrence let the developer answer the same
+      // value two different ways, and only the last answer took effect — for every occurrence, silently.
+      W._wallReset();
+      wallAsked.length = 0;
+      const snapDup = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+      // The same value twice must share ONE token id — otherwise the agent cannot tell the header and the row
+      // are the same person, and a single reveal would clear only one of them.
+      const spans = snapDup.match(/wall:[0-9a-f]{16}/g) || [];
+      const distinctToks = [...new Set(spans)];
+      ok(!/482-11-9037/.test(snapDup), 'E2-8: both occurrences of the SSN are masked');
+      ok(spans.length >= 2, `E2-9: each occurrence is replaced in place (${spans.length} spans)`);
+      ok(distinctToks.length < spans.length, `E2-10: repeated values share ONE token id (${distinctToks.length} ids for ${spans.length} spans)`);
+      ok(/matched a sensitive-data pattern/.test(snapDup), 'E2-11: the agent is told, once, in the result itself');
+
+      wallAnswer = 'allow';
+      W._wallReset();
+    }
+    // ── [P] a value ANOTHER plugin already protects must not be re-discovered here ────────────────────────
+    // The pii plugin knows real values by heart. If the wall scanned first, a known SSN would leave as a
+    // TEMPORARY wall token and the developer would be prompted about a value the system already protects.
+    // So the wall pre-masks through the platform's content filters, then only asks about what is left.
+    console.log('\n[P] pre-mask: known values keep their stable token');
+    {
+      const W = await import('../examples/plugins/browserdebug__1.mjs');
+      W._wallReset();
+      wallAsked.length = 0;
+      wallAnswer = 'mask';
+      // Stand in for the pii filter: it knows 482-11-9037 and nothing else. The fuzzy form deliberately embeds a
+      // 16-hex id, which carries a 10-digit run — exactly the artefact the wall must NOT ask about.
+      preMask = (t) => t.replace(/482-11-9037/g, '@@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn)')
+        .replace(/1002003004/g, '###{edi across 2 families #0123456789abcdef}');
+
+      const snapP = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+      ok(/@@@\(smiths\.sponsor\.ssn\)/.test(snapP), `P1: the known SSN leaves as the OTHER plugin's stable token (${(snapP.match(/@@@\([^)]*\)/) || [''])[0]})`);
+      ok(!/482-11-9037/.test(snapP), 'P2: and its real value never reaches the agent');
+      const askedVals = JSON.stringify(wallAsked).match(/482-11-9037/g) || [];
+      ok(askedVals.length === 0, 'P3: the developer is NOT prompted about a value the system already protects');
+      ok(!/0123456789/.test(JSON.stringify(wallAsked)), 'P4: nor about digits that exist only INSIDE another plugin\'s token (a masking artefact)');
+      ok(!/wall:[0-9a-f]{16}/.test(snapP), 'P5: no temporary wall token stands in for a value that has a permanent one');
+
+      // An UNKNOWN value in the same result is still caught — pre-masking must not blunt the wall.
+      preMask = (t) => t.replace(/482-11-9037/g, '@@@(smiths.sponsor.ssn)');
+      W._wallReset(); wallAsked.length = 0;
+      await call.browser_navigate({ label: 'dbg', url: base + '/?u=1' });
+      const snapU = await call.browser_snapshot({ label: 'dbg' });
+      ok(wallAsked.length === 0, 'P6: an UNKNOWN value is masked SILENTLY — no prompt, like everything else');
+      ok(!/1002003004/.test(snapU) && /wall:[0-9a-f]{16}/.test(snapU), 'P7: and gets a temporary wall token');
+
+      preMask = (t) => t;
+      wallAnswer = 'allow';
+      W._wallReset();
+    }
+    console.log('\n[G] close');
+    ok(/closed/.test(await call.browser_close({ label: 'dbg' })), 'G1: session closes');
+    ok(!/dbg:/.test(await call.browser_list({})), 'G2: it is gone from the list');
+
+    // Node ships a global WebSocket only from v21, so on Node 18/20 the plugin used to die at connect with
+    // "WebSocket is not defined". CB_BROWSERDEBUG_WS=shim forces the built-in RFC-6455 fallback, so this proves the
+    // fallback drives a REAL Chrome over a REAL CDP session on any Node — including payloads past both extended
+    // length forms (>125 and >64KiB) in each direction, where the framing is easiest to get wrong.
+    console.log('\n[H] the built-in WebSocket fallback (forced) drives the same session');
+    process.env.CB_BROWSERDEBUG_WS = 'shim';
+    try {
+      const o2 = await call.browser_open({ label: 'shim', headless: true, url: base + '/' });
+      ok(!/THREW/.test(o2), `H1: opened over the fallback socket (${o2.slice(0, 60)})`);
+      if (/THREW/.test(o2)) throw new Error('shim open failed: ' + o2);
+      ok((await call.browser_eval({ label: 'shim', js: 'window.appState.count' })).trim() === '0', 'H2: CDP request/response round-trips');
+      const big = await call.browser_eval({ label: 'shim', js: '"x".repeat(200000).length' });   // >64KiB frame INBOUND
+      ok(big.trim() === '200000', `H3: a >64KiB inbound frame arrives intact (${big.trim()})`);
+      // The marker is deliberately NON-numeric: the payload length (70000) is five digits, which the wall masks
+      // as a possible ZIP. That is the ZIP rule working as specified; it just made a bad assertion here.
+      const outbound = await call.browser_eval({ label: 'shim', js: `["${'y'.repeat(70000)}"][0].length === 70000 ? "BIGFRAME_OK" : "BAD"` });   // >64KiB OUTBOUND
+      ok(outbound.trim() === 'BIGFRAME_OK', `H4: a >64KiB outbound frame is framed + masked correctly (${outbound.trim()})`);
+      await sleep(250);
+      ok(/page loaded v1/.test(await call.browser_console({ label: 'shim' })), 'H5: buffered CDP events stream over the fallback');
+      ok(/closed/.test(await call.browser_close({ label: 'shim' })), 'H6: fallback session closes cleanly');
+    } finally { delete process.env.CB_BROWSERDEBUG_WS; }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.stack || e); }
+  finally {
+    try { await plugin.dispose(); } catch {}
+    for (const [id] of children) host.killSpawned(id);
+    server.close();
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

+/**
+ * The budget monitor (the developer, 2026-10-13: "do we know what time the tokens replenish?" - we did not; the
+ * endpoint's reset header is a constant). It notes the budget from every reply and says what the rises look like.
+ * Run: node test/test-budget-monitor.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { createBudgetMonitor } from '../src/budget-monitor.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-budget-'));
+const MIN = 60_000, HOUR = 3600_000, LIMIT = 100_000_000;
+const T0 = new Date(2026, 9, 13, 9, 0, 0).getTime();
+const b = (t, remaining) => ({ at: t, credits: { limit: LIMIT, remaining, resetAt: t + 90000 * 1000 }, tokens: { limit: 1_000_000, remaining: 990_000 } });
+
+console.log('\n[1] nothing seen');
+{
+  const m = createBudgetMonitor({ file: path.join(dir, 'none.jsonl') });
+  ok(m.report().seen === 0 && /No budget figure has been seen yet/.test(m.report().text), 'says so');
+  m.note({ at: T0, credits: null }); m.note(null);
+  ok(m.report().seen === 0, 'a reply with no credit figure is not a reading');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] only ever falling');
+{
+  let now = T0; const f = path.join(dir, 'fall.jsonl');
+  const m = createBudgetMonitor({ file: f, now: () => now });
+  for (let i = 0; i <= 180; i++) { now = T0 + i * 20_000; m.note(b(now, 60_000_000 - i * 100_000)); }   // three calls a minute for an hour
+  const lines = (await readFile(f, 'utf8')).trim().split('\n').length;
+  ok(lines >= 55 && lines <= 62, `one line a minute at most, however many calls (${lines} lines for 181 calls)`);
+  const r = m.report();
+  ok(/has not gone up once/.test(r.text) && r.rises.length === 0, 'no rise seen: says the moment has not been seen yet');
+  ok(/Credits left: 42,[0-9,]+ of 100,000,000 \(4[23]% left\)/.test(r.text), `what is left, of what (${r.text.split('\n')[1]})`);
+  ok(/Spent in the last hour: 1[78],[0-9,]+ - at that rate what is left lasts about 2 h/.test(r.text), `the rate, and how long it lasts (${r.text.split('\n')[2]})`);
+  ok(/has read: 90000 every time - a constant, not a countdown/.test(r.text), 'the reset header\'s constant is called what it is');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] a reset at a fixed moment');
+{
+  let now = T0; const m = createBudgetMonitor({ file: path.join(dir, 'reset.jsonl'), now: () => now });
+  for (let i = 0; i < 30; i++) { now = T0 + i * 2 * MIN; m.note(b(now, 30_000_000 - i * 500_000)); }
+  now += 2 * MIN; m.note(b(now, 99_800_000));   // two minutes later it is nearly full
+  now += 2 * MIN; m.note(b(now, 99_500_000));
+  const r = m.report();
+  ok(r.rises.length === 1 && r.rises[0].gained > 80_000_000, 'the rise is recorded');
+  ok(/a RESET at a fixed moment: between 2026-10-13 09:58 and 2026-10-13 10:00/.test(r.text), `and read as a reset, with the two readings it fell between (${r.text.split('\n').find((l) => /RESET/.test(l))})`);
+  ok(/Each time it went up:\n {2}2026-10-13 09:58 -> 2026-10-13 10:00 \(2 min apart\)/.test(r.text), 'each rise is listed');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[4] across a gap, and a rolling window');
+{
+  let now = T0; const m = createBudgetMonitor({ file: path.join(dir, 'gap.jsonl'), now: () => now });
+  m.note(b(now, 20_000_000)); now += 9 * HOUR; m.note(b(now, 95_000_000));
+  ok(/across a gap with no readings/.test(m.report().text), 'a big rise over hours with nothing between is not called a reset');
+  let n2 = T0; const w = createBudgetMonitor({ file: path.join(dir, 'roll.jsonl'), now: () => n2 });
+  let left = 50_000_000;
+  for (let i = 0; i < 40; i++) { n2 = T0 + i * 10 * MIN; left += i % 4 === 0 ? 400_000 : -150_000; w.note(b(n2, left)); }
+  ok(/ROLLING window/.test(w.report().text), 'small amounts coming back again and again read as a rolling window');
+  // a stale header: a few thousand higher than the last reading is not credits coming back
+  let n3 = T0; const j = createBudgetMonitor({ file: path.join(dir, 'jit.jsonl'), now: () => n3 });
+  j.note(b(n3, 10_000_000)); n3 += 2 * MIN; j.note(b(n3, 10_004_000)); n3 += 2 * MIN; j.note(b(n3, 9_990_000));
+  ok(j.report().rises.length === 0, 'a late reading a few thousand higher is not a rise');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[5] two windows, one file');
+{
+  const f = path.join(dir, 'two.jsonl'); let now = T0;
+  const a = createBudgetMonitor({ file: f, now: () => now }), c = createBudgetMonitor({ file: f, now: () => now });
+  a.note(b(now, 9_000_000)); now += 30_000; c.note(b(now, 8_500_000)); now += 5 * MIN; a.note(b(now, 8_000_000));
+  ok(c.report().seen === 3 && a.report().last.remaining === 8_000_000, 'both write, and either reports all of it');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[6] what the gateway says beside the figure (docs/api_docs)');
+{
+  let now = T0; const m = createBudgetMonitor({ file: path.join(dir, 'notes.jsonl'), now: () => now });
+  m.note(b(now, 9_000_000));
+  for (let i = 1; i <= 5; i++) { now = T0 + i * 20_000; m.note({ ...b(now, 9_000_000), recordingFailed: true }); }
+  now = T0 + 3 * MIN; m.note({ ...b(now, 9_000_000), notices: ['warning: 299 - "alias is deprecated; use gemini-3.8-flash"', 'sunset: Sat, 12 Dec 2026'] });
+  const r = m.report();
+  ok(/usage recording had failed on 5 replies in the last 24 hours/.test(r.text) && /stands still and is behind/.test(r.text), 'replies that said usage recording failed are counted, with what that means for the figure');

+await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});

=== add test/test-budget-pie-e2e.mjs
new 00da10d80949f53b8ebc513f
+/**
+ * The status-bar budget pie (the developer, 2026-10-12: "instead of a token count, in the bottom status bar, let's
+ * put a little pie describing how much of the token budget is left"). The gateway states its budget on every reply
+ * (probe 9h: x-ratelimit-limit/remaining/reset for credits-day and tokens-minute).
+ *   [1] parseBudgetHeaders reads them; nothing there = null
+ *   [2] the real HTTP adapter + server + page against a stand-in gateway sending exactly those headers: the pie
+ *       replaces the counter, filled to what is left, and its tooltip says the numbers
+ *   [3] it survives a reload (the server keeps the last budget)
+ *   [4] a gateway that states no budget: the counter stays, no pie
+ * Run: node test/test-budget-pie-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtempSync, writeFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import http from 'node:http';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+process.env.CB_HOME = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-budget-home-'));
+const { startServer, parseBudgetHeaders } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const chrome = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe', (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+
+const HEADERS = {
+  'x-ratelimit-limit-credits-day': '100000000', 'x-ratelimit-remaining-credits-day': '69654901', 'x-ratelimit-reset-credits-day': '88806',
+  'x-ratelimit-limit-tokens-minute': '1000000', 'x-ratelimit-remaining-tokens-minute': '989297', 'x-ratelimit-reset-tokens-minute': '43',
+};
+
+console.log('\n[1] reading the headers');
+{
+  const b = parseBudgetHeaders(HEADERS, 1000);
+  ok(b && b.credits.limit === 100000000 && b.credits.remaining === 69654901 && b.credits.resetAt === 1000 + 88806000, 'the daily credits, with the reset as a time');
+  ok(b && b.tokens.limit === 1000000 && b.tokens.remaining === 989297, 'and the per-minute tokens');
+  ok(parseBudgetHeaders({ 'content-type': 'x' }) === null && parseBudgetHeaders(null) === null, 'no budget headers = null');
+}
+
+// A stand-in for the gateway: OpenAI-shaped, streaming, with or without the budget headers.
+const gateway = (withBudget) => http.createServer((req, res) => {
+  let body = ''; req.on('data', (c) => { body += c; });
+  req.on('end', () => {
+    const extra = withBudget ? HEADERS : {};
+    if (req.url.endsWith('/models')) { res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json', ...extra }); res.end(JSON.stringify({ data: [{ id: 'm' }] })); return; }
+    let j = {}; try { j = JSON.parse(body); } catch {}
+    if (j.stream) {
+      res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/event-stream', ...extra });
+      res.write('data: ' + JSON.stringify({ choices: [{ index: 0, delta: { content: 'Hello there.' } }] }) + '\n\n');
+      res.write('data: ' + JSON.stringify({ choices: [{ index: 0, delta: {}, finish_reason: 'stop' }], usage: { prompt_tokens: 10, completion_tokens: 3, total_tokens: 13 } }) + '\n\n');
+      res.end('data: [DONE]\n\n');
+    } else { res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'application/json', ...extra }); res.end(JSON.stringify({ choices: [{ index: 0, message: { role: 'assistant', content: 'ok' }, finish_reason: 'stop' }] })); }
+  });
+});
+
+async function session(withBudget, port, gwPort) {
+  const gw = gateway(withBudget); await new Promise((r) => gw.listen(gwPort, '127.0.0.1', r));
+  const proj = mkdtempSync(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-budget-proj-'));
+  writeFileSync(path.join(proj, 'a.txt'), 'x\n');
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8')).replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: 'x' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html, baseURL: `http://127.0.0.1:${gwPort}/v1`, apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: proj });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  const page = await browser.newPage();
+  const errs = []; page.on('pageerror', (e) => errs.push(e.message));
+  await page.setViewport({ width: 1200, height: 800 });
+  await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${port}/`);
+  await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+  await page.type('#input', 'hi');
+  await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+  await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__sessionRef?.status === 'done' || window.__sessionRef?.status === 'idle', { timeout: 20000 }).catch(() => {});
+  await sleep(600);
+  const read = () => page.evaluate(() => {
+    const p = document.getElementById('budgetPie'), c = document.getElementById('tokenCounter');
+    return { pie: p && p.style.display !== 'none', p: p && p.style.getPropertyValue('--p'), title: p && p.title, cls: p && p.className, counter: c && c.style.display !== 'none' && !!c.textContent };
+  });
+  return { page, browser, server, gw, errs, read };
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP - Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+  let s = null;
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[2] a gateway that states its budget');
+    s = await session(true, 29591, 29592);
+    const a = await s.read();
+    if (process.env.CB_SHOT) {   // a picture of the status bar, to look at the pie
+      const box = await s.page.evaluate(() => { const r = document.getElementById('budgetPie').parentElement.getBoundingClientRect(); return { x: Math.max(0, r.x - 10), y: Math.max(0, r.y - 8), width: Math.min(r.width + 20, 1180), height: r.height + 16 }; });
+      await s.page.screenshot({ path: process.env.CB_SHOT, clip: box });
+    }
+    ok(a.pie && !a.counter, `the pie shows, in the counter's place (pie=${a.pie} counter=${a.counter})`);
+    ok(Math.abs(Number(a.p) - 69.7) < 0.1 && !/low|out/.test(a.cls), `filled to what is left: ${a.p}%, not marked low`);
+    ok(/Daily credits: 69,654,901 of 100,000,000 left \(70%\) — resets in 24 h 4\d min/.test(a.title || ''), `the tooltip says the daily credits and when they reset (${(a.title || '').split('\n')[0]})`);
+    ok(/This minute: 989,297 of 1,000,000 tokens left/.test(a.title || ''), 'and this minute\'s tokens');
+
+    console.log('\n[3] after a reload');
+    await s.page.reload();
+    await s.page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+    await sleep(800);
+    const b = await s.read();
+    ok(b.pie && Math.abs(Number(b.p) - 69.7) < 0.1, 'still there, from the server\'s last copy');
+    ok(s.errs.length === 0, `no page errors (${s.errs.slice(0, 2).join(' | ')})`);
+    await s.browser.close(); s.server.close(); s.gw.close(); s = null;
+
+    console.log('\n[4] a gateway that states none');
+    s = await session(false, 29593, 29594);
+    const c = await s.read();
+    ok(!c.pie && c.counter, `no pie; the conversation counter stays (pie=${c.pie} counter=${c.counter})`);
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { if (s) { try { await s.browser.close(); } catch {} try { s.server.close(); } catch {} try { s.gw.close(); } catch {} } }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

=== add test/test-bundle-names.mjs
new 79fd6d2d8a034ba7227b7242
+/**
+ * EVERY NAME A BUNDLED MODULE IMPORTS IS THERE IN THE BUNDLE (prod 2596: `export { sEvNum as streamEvNum }` was dropped
+ * by the bundler, so the bookkeeper's pass and historyAsk threw "streamEvNum is not defined" on the box - and the tests,
+ * which run the sources, never saw it).
+ *   [1] each `import { a, b } from './x.mjs'` of a module in dist/code-boss.mjs names something declared at the bundle's
+ *       top level (a function, class, const, let or var - or a header import)
+ *   [2] the two names behind the bug are declared
+ *   [3] the bundler turns a renamed export into a name, and refuses a renamed import of its own module
+ * Needs the bundle: run node scripts/build.mjs first (SKIP otherwise). Run: node test/test-bundle-names.mjs
+ */
+import { readFileSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+
+const ROOT = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), '..');
+const DIST = path.join(ROOT, 'dist', 'code-boss.mjs');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+if (!existsSync(DIST)) { console.log('SKIP - no dist/code-boss.mjs (run node scripts/build.mjs)'); process.exit(0); }
+const bundle = readFileSync(DIST, 'utf8').replace(/\r\n/g, '\n');
+const declared = new Set();
+for (const m of bundle.matchAll(/^(?:async\s+)?function\s*\*?\s*([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)/gm)) declared.add(m[1]);
+for (const m of bundle.matchAll(/^(?:const|let|var|class)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)/gm)) declared.add(m[1]);
+for (const m of bundle.matchAll(/^(?:const|let|var)\s*\{([^}]*)\}\s*=/gm)) for (const n of m[1].split(',')) { const x = n.split(':').pop().split('=')[0].trim(); if (x) declared.add(x); }
+for (const m of bundle.matchAll(/^import\s*\{([^}]*)\}\s*from/gm)) for (const n of m[1].split(',')) { const x = n.split(/\s+as\s+/).pop().trim(); if (x) declared.add(x); }
+for (const m of bundle.matchAll(/^import\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*(?:,|from)/gm)) declared.add(m[1]);
+for (const m of bundle.matchAll(/^import\s*\*\s*as\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)/gm)) declared.add(m[1]);
+
+console.log('\n[1] every imported name is declared in the bundle');
+// (a module's marker names its path under src/: "// ====== agent/stream.mjs ======")
+const files = [...bundle.matchAll(/^\/\/ ====== (?:injected from )?(?:src\/)?([\w./-]+\.mjs) ======$/gm)].map((m) => 'src/' + m[1]);
+const missing = [];
+let names = 0;
+for (const rel of new Set(files)) {
+  const abs = path.join(ROOT, rel);
+  if (!existsSync(abs)) continue;
+  const src = readFileSync(abs, 'utf8');
+  for (const im of src.matchAll(/^import\s*\{([^}]*)\}\s*from\s*['"](\.\.?\/[^'"]+)['"]/gm)) {
+    for (const n of im[1].split(',').map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean)) {
+      names++;
+      if (!declared.has(n)) missing.push(`${rel}: ${n} (from ${im[2]})`);
+    }
+  }
+}
+ok(files.length > 20 && names > 200, `read the bundle's modules (${new Set(files).size}) and the names they import (${names})`);
+ok(!missing.length, `every one is declared at the bundle's top level${missing.length ? ' - missing: ' + missing.slice(0, 10).join('; ') : ''}`);
+
+console.log('\n[2] the names behind the prod bug');
+ok(declared.has('streamEvNum') && declared.has('streamUnitText'), 'streamEvNum and streamUnitText are declared (const streamEvNum = sEvNum)');
+
+console.log('\n[3] the bundler itself');
+const build = readFileSync(path.join(ROOT, 'scripts', 'build.mjs'), 'utf8');
+ok(/const \$\{m\[2\]\} = \$\{m\[1\]\};/.test(build) && /a renamed import of a bundled module has no name in the flat bundle/.test(build), 'a renamed export becomes a const; a renamed import of our own module stops the build');
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);
+

new 6933025232d7432ec943e9af
+// Guards the PACKAGED path, which is the one that broke: the probe worked in src/ and every test passed, then the
+// button failed on a real install with
+//
+//   Probe failed: Cannot find module 'C:\Users\...\.code_boss\app\behaviour-ladder.mjs'
+//
+// because the runner reached the ladder through `await import('./behaviour-ladder.mjs')`. Static imports are
+// inlined by the flat-scope concatenation; a dynamic one survives as a literal path and resolves at RUNTIME against
+// the install directory, where no source tree exists. Nothing that tested src/ could see it — so this file tests
+// dist/ instead. It is cheap static analysis, not a boot: the bundle self-starts and exports nothing.
+import { readFile, stat } from 'node:fs/promises';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+
+const BUNDLE = new URL('../dist/code-boss.mjs', import.meta.url);
+try { await stat(BUNDLE); } catch { console.log('SKIP: dist/code-boss.mjs not built'); process.exit(0); }
+const bundle = await readFile(BUNDLE, 'utf8');
+
+// Comments are dropped: the comment explaining this trap contains the pattern being searched for.
+const codeOnly = bundle.split('\n').filter((l) => { const t = l.trim(); return !(t.startsWith('//') || t.startsWith('*') || t.startsWith('/*')); }).join('\n');
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[1] no relative dynamic import survives into the bundle\n');
+{
+  const dyn = [...codeOnly.matchAll(/\bimport\s*\(\s*['"`](\.[^'"`]*)['"`]/g)].map((m) => m[1]);
+  ok(dyn.length === 0, `none found${dyn.length ? ' — ' + [...new Set(dyn)].join(', ') : ''}`);
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[2] every probe entry point is actually IN the bundle\n');
+{
+  // If a module stops being bundled, or a symbol is renamed on one side only, the endpoint throws at click time on
+  // a user's machine. These are the exact names the request handler calls.
+  for (const sym of ['runBehaviourLadder', 'formatLadderReport', 'buildLadder', 'buildLadderContext', 'scoreLadder',
+    'probeBuildHistory', 'probeCompactGenerations', 'probeCall', 'probePrompts', 'probeToolAsks', 'runModelProbe', 'formatProbeReport',
+    'runEnvProbe', 'formatEnvProbeReport', 'envProbeQuestions', 'envProbeDerive']) {
+    ok(new RegExp(`(function|const|let|var)\\s+${sym}\\b`).test(codeOnly), `${sym} is declared in the bundle`);
+  }
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[3] the endpoint is wired to them\n');
+{
+  ok(/['"]\/api\/probe-model['"]/.test(codeOnly), 'the /api/probe-model route is present');
+  ok(/runBehaviourLadder\s*\(\s*\{/.test(codeOnly), 'the route calls runBehaviourLadder');
+  ok(/formatLadderReport\s*\(/.test(codeOnly), 'the route formats the ladder report');
+  // Both buttons go through one polled job. The preset has to survive to the server or a click silently runs
+  // the wrong probe — the older cell sweep, or the ladder instead of the environment questions.
+  ok(/runEnvProbe\s*\(\s*\{/.test(codeOnly), 'the route calls runEnvProbe');
+  ok(/formatEnvProbeReport\s*\(/.test(codeOnly), 'the route formats the environment report');
+  ok(/'standard',\s*'quick',\s*'env'/.test(codeOnly), "the route accepts the 'env' preset");
+  // The Settings UI that used to send this is gone; the debug page sends it now, and the preset is still the
+  // thing that decides which probe runs, so it still has to survive into the bundle.
+  ok(/JSON\.stringify\(\{ preset: p\.preset/.test(bundle) || /body\.preset/.test(codeOnly), 'the debug page forwards the chosen preset to the server');
+  for (const sym of ['runProdSpec', 'formatProdSpecReport', 'buildProdSpec', 'SPEC_LAYERS']) {
+    ok(new RegExp(`(function|const|let|var)\\s+${sym}\\b`).test(codeOnly), `${sym} is declared in the bundle`);
+  }
+
+  // The dev session rides the same polled job as the other three, so the whole cost of a fourth probe is one
+  // branch on the server and one button in the UI. What it must NOT share is the partial-report behaviour: the
+  // others keep progress in the settings pane because a scoreboard that redraws cannot be photographed, and this
+  // one streams to the full page because a nine-turn transcript is unreadable in a small box.
+  ok(/'env',\s*'spec',\s*'session'/.test(codeOnly), "the route accepts the 'session' preset");
+  ok(/runDevSession\s*\(\s*\{/.test(codeOnly), 'the route calls runDevSession');
+  ok(/formatDevSessionReport\s*\(/.test(codeOnly), 'and formats the transcript');
+  ok(/onTurn:/.test(codeOnly), 'publishing after every turn, so a multi-minute session is readable while it runs');
+  for (const sym of ['runDevSession', 'formatDevSessionReport', 'devSessionScript', 'DEV_SESSION_PROMPT']) {
+    ok(new RegExp(`(function|const|let|var)\\s+${sym}\\b`).test(codeOnly), `${sym} is declared in the bundle`);
+  }
+  ok(/outEl\.innerHTML = buf\.join/.test(bundle), 'the debug page appends to ONE document as the run goes, rather than waiting for the end');
+  // ONE BUTTON, and it is now the DEBUG page rather than a probe button in Settings. Five buttons became one,
+  // then that one merged with the diagnostics entry: probes and snapshot queries answer the same question and
+  // share a number space. What must hold in the PACKAGED bundle is that the page and its route survive the
+  // concatenation, and that no second probe entry has grown back beside it.
+  ok(/debugPageHtml/.test(codeOnly), 'the debug page builder is in the bundle');
+  ok(/'\/debug'/.test(codeOnly) && /'\/snapshot'/.test(codeOnly), 'and both routes resolve — /snapshot is bookmarked and an open tab still asks for it');
+  ok(/openDebug\(\)/.test(bundle), 'the UI reaches it');
+  ok(!/planProbeBtn/.test(bundle), 'and the old Settings probe button is gone, not merely hidden — two entries to the same thing is the duplication this removed')

+/**
+ * PLUGINS THAT SHIP WITH THE APP.
+ *
+ * The PII test-data plugin is what keeps real values away from the model, so it cannot be an optional extra a
+ * developer copies in by hand — it has to be present before the first message, on a fresh install and after an
+ * update. The bundle carries its source; the server writes it into ~/.code_boss/plugins at start-up.
+ *
+ * The rule that makes that safe to do on EVERY start: never clobber a local edit. We record the hash of what we
+ * wrote; a later start overwrites only a file still matching that hash. A copy the developer has changed is left
+ * exactly as it is.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-bundled-plugins.mjs
+ */

=== add test/test-busy-send-ux.mjs
new 53be1fc0c56e70258ebfe017
+/**
+ * TYPING WHILE IT WORKS IS NOT AN ERROR.
+ *
+ * Found by driving a real session: send a follow-up while the agent is mid-run and you get a red error dialog
+ * reading
+ *
+ *   Send failed × ERR Send failed  msg HTTP 409 — {"error":"chat is already running"}  proj C:/Users/…
+ *
+ * for the most ordinary thing a developer does. The HTTP status, the raw JSON body and the project path are all
+ * in there. And 409 is simply the correct answer now: the background sub-agent system that used to absorb a
+ * busy-time message was removed, so this is not a rare race, it is what happens every time.
+ *
+ * Drives the real UI against a real server with a run genuinely in flight.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-busy-send-ux.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-busy-home-'));
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29293;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const SEP = String.fromCharCode(92);
+const chrome = [
+  'C:/Program Files/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+  (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '').split(SEP).join('/') + '/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+  'C:/Program Files (x86)/Google/Chrome/Application/chrome.exe',
+].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+
+/** Streams slowly enough that the second message definitely lands mid-run. */
+const slowAdapter = {
+  async *completeStream() { for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) { await sleep(150); yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: 'working… ' } }] }; } },
+  async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'ok' } }] }; },
+};
+
+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP: Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-busy-'));
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8'))
+    .replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+    defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter: slowAdapter, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir,
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1200, height: 900 });
+    await page.goto('http://127.0.0.1:' + PORT + '/');
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 20000 });
+
+    console.log('\n[1] a run is genuinely in flight');
+    await page.type('#input', 'first message, this one takes a while');
+    await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => (window.__sessionRef || {}).status === 'running', { timeout: 15000 });
+    ok(true, 'the agent is working');
+
+    console.log('\n[2] typing a follow-up does NOT raise an error dialog');
+    const SECOND = 'also update the README while you are at it';
+    await page.type('#input', SECOND);
+    await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+    await sleep(2500);
+    const seen = await page.evaluate(() => {
+      const vis = (el) => el && el.offsetParent !== null;
+      const modals = [...document.querySelectorAll('.modal-bg, [id$="ModalBg"], [class*="error"]')]
+        .filter(vis).map((e) => (e.innerText || '').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim());
+      const toasts = [...document.querySelectorAll('[class*="toast"]')].filter(vis).map((e) => (e.innerText || '').trim());
+      return { modals, toasts, input: document.getElementById('input').value, status: (window.__sessionRef || {}).status };
+    });
+    ok(!seen.modals.some((m) => /Send failed/i.test(m)), `no "Send failed" dialog (${JSON.stringify(seen.modals).slice(0, 120)})`);
+    // The specific leaks that made the old dialog bad: the developer should never be shown an HTTP status, a raw
+    // JSON body, or their own filesystem path for typing too soon.
+    const all = seen.modals.join(' ');
+    ok(!/\b409\b/.test(all), 'no HTTP status code shown');
+    ok(!/\{"error"/.test(all), 'no raw JSON body shown');
+    ok(!/[A-Za-z]:\//.test(all), 'no filesystem path shown');
+
+    console.log('\n[3] it STEERS: the message is shown at once and reaches the agent at its next step');
+    // Since build 1960 Enter mid-run steers (it used to 409, keep the text in the box and show a "still working"
+    // toast — so a developer's "also do X" waited for the whole run to end). The text must not be lost either way.
+    const pending = await page.evaluate(() => [...document.querySelectorAll('.pending-steer')].map((e) => e.innerText));
+    ok(pending.some((t) => t.includes(SECOND)), `the message is shown in the chat, marked pending (${JSON.stringify(pending).slice(0, 80)})`);
+    ok(seen.input === '', `the input box is cleared (${JSON.stringify(seen.input).slice(0, 60)})`);
+
+    console.log('\n[4] the run itself is untouched');
+    ok(seen.status === 'running', 'the in-flight run was not cancelled or disturbed');
+
+    console.log('\n[5] a REAL send failure still raises the dialog');
+    {
+      // The quiet path must be narrow: only "already running". A server that is down, or any other failure, must
+      // still be loud — muffling those would be a much worse bug than the one being fixed.
+      await page.evaluate(() => { window.fetch = () => Promise.reject(new Error('boom')); });
+      await page.evaluate(() => { const i = document.getElementById('input'); i.value = ''; i.focus(); });
+      await page.type('#input', 'this should fail loudly');
+      await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+      await sleep(1500);
+      const loud = await page.evaluate(() => [...document.querySelectorAll('.modal-bg, [id$="ModalBg"], [class*="error"]')]
+        .filter((e) => e.offsetParent !== null).map((e) => (e.innerText || '').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim()).join(' '));
+      ok(/(Send|Steer) failed/i.test(loud), `a genuine failure still shows the dialog (${loud.slice(0, 90)})`);
+      ok((await page.evaluate(() => document.getElementById('input').value)) === 'this should fail loudly', 'and the text is back in the box');
+    }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { await browser.close().catch(() => {}); server.close(); await rm(projectDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+
+  console.log('\n' + pass + ' passed, ' + fail + ' failed');
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();
+
=== add test/test-call-log.mjs
new 22c03c44154bbb64f22368ae
+/**
+ * WHAT A SNIPPET DID — the model sees every call's result, in order, after the snippet's result (build 1963).
+ *
+ * A snippet is the agent continuing its turn on the client. It used to get back only the value the snippet
+ * returned, so an agent whose 30-step script went wrong at step 2 had to guess what happened (in production it
+ * rewrote whole scripts over and over). Now the result is followed by the record: each call with what it returned
+ * or why it failed, console.log lines where they ran, parallel() branches in their own sections. Capped (8 KB a
+ * call, 64 KB a snippet); the complete record goes to .code_boss/logs/<callId>.log, masked.
+ *
+ *   [1] a failing snippet: the error, then the record — calls in order with their results, the console line
+ *       where it ran, the failing call marked "the error above"; nothing cut, so no pointer to the log
+ *   [2] a big result is in the record by its size (2026-10-28: only small values are shown), and the model is pointed at the log, which has all of it
+ *   [3] past the snippet budget, the remaining calls shrink to one line each
+ *   [4] masked: a card number in a read result and in a console line is a token in the record and the log
+ *   [5] parallel(): each branch in its own section; a failing branch is named, the other one still finished
+ *   [6] a non-zero exit THROWS with the end of the output; {allowFail: true} returns it as data
+ *   [7] the agent can read() its own log
+ *   [8] the UI: expanding a finished snippet's result shows the complete record
+ * Run: node test/test-call-log.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { runMinimalWorker } from '../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const C = '/--- you ab12 ---';
+const CARD = '4111 1111 1111 1111';
+const block = (js) => `<remote-workspace><js>${js}</js></remote-workspace>`;
+
+async function run(dir, snippets, { filter } = {}) {
+  let i = 0;
+  const replies = [...snippets.map(block), 'Done.\n' + C];
+  const adapter = { async *completeStream() { yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: replies[Math.min(i++, replies.length - 1)] } }] }; } };
+  const events = [];
+  await runMinimalWorker({ adapter, model: 't', signal: { aborted: false }, messages: [{ content: 'go' }], projectDir: dir,
+    requireBranch: () => 'ok', requireApproval: () => true, onChatEvent: (e) => events.push(e), onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {},
+    ...(filter ? { contentFilter: filter } : {}) });
+  return events.filter((e) => e.type === 'tool-result' && e.name === 'js_eval');
+}
+const recordOf = (r) => { const s = String(r.content || ''); const i = s.indexOf('\n\nWhat the snippet did, in order:\n'); return i < 0 ? '' : s.slice(i); };
+const logText = async (dir, r) => (r.logPath ? readFile(path.join(dir, r.logPath), 'utf8').catch(() => null) : null);
+
+const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-runrec-'));
+await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'a.txt'), 'hello');
+await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'big.txt'), 'x'.repeat(30000) + 'END_OF_BIG');
+await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'card.txt'), 'card on file: ' + CARD);
+await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'fail.js'), 'console.log("partial work"); console.error("the real reason"); process.exit(4)');
+
+console.log('\n[1] a failing snippet');
+{
+  const [r] = await run(dir, ['const t = await read("a.txt"); console.log("got", t.length, "chars"); await read("missing.txt"); return "never"']);
+  const rec = recordOf(r);
+  ok(r.status === 'error' && /^ERROR: /.test(r.content) && /missing\.txt/.test(r.content.split('\n')[0]), `the error comes first (${String(r.content).split('\n')[0].slice(0, 70)})`);
+  ok(/\[1\] read\(a\.txt\) · \d+ms → hello/.test(rec), 'the record has the first call WITH its result');
+  const iRead = rec.indexOf('[1] read(a.txt)'), iLog = rec.indexOf('console: got 5 chars'), iFail = rec.indexOf('[2] read(missing.txt)');
+  ok(iRead >= 0 && iLog > iRead && iFail > iLog, `in the order it happened: call, console line, failing call (${[iRead, iLog, iFail].join(',')})`);
+  ok(/\[2\] read\(missing\.txt\) · \d+ms → FAILED — the error above/.test(rec), 'the failing call points at the error instead of repeating it');
+  ok(!/complete record/.test(r.content), 'nothing was cut, so no pointer to the log');
+  const lg = await logText(dir, r);
+  ok(lg && /\[1\] read\(a\.txt\)/.test(lg) && /--- ERROR ---/.test(lg), `the log file has the record and the error (${r.logPath})`);
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] a big result');
+{
+  const [r] = await run(dir, ['const b = await read("big.txt"); return b.length']);
+  const rec = recordOf(r);
+  ok(/^30010\b/.test(r.content), 'the result is the value');
+  // (2026-10-28: the record carries a call's value only when it is small - the snippet's return value is what it wanted)
+  ok(rec.length < 400 && /\[1\] read\(big\.txt\)[^\n]* → ok · \d+ lines?/.test(rec) && !/END_OF_BIG|xxxx/.test(rec), `the read is in the record by its size, not its text (${rec.length} chars: ${rec.split('\n')[0]})`);
+  ok(new RegExp("Every call's whole result, and every console line, is in " + String(r.logPath).replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\/]/g, '\\$&')).test(r.content), 'and the model is pointed at the log');
+  const lg = await logText(dir, r);
+  ok(lg && /END_OF_BIG/.test(lg), 'which has all of it');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] twelve big reads');
+{
+  // (before 2026-10-28 each was in the record cut to 8 KB, then to a line past 64 KB - the same text over and over)
+  const [r] = await run(dir, ['for (let i = 0; i < 12; i++) await read("big.txt"); return "done"']);
+  const rec = recordOf(r);
+  ok(rec.length < 2000 && !/END_OF_BIG|xxxx/.test(rec), `twelve lines of status, none of their text (${rec.length} chars; ${(rec.match(/\n\[\d+\]/g) || []).length + 1} calls listed)`);
+  ok(/\[12\] read\(big\.txt\)[^\n]* → ok · /.test(rec), 'every call is still listed, with how it ended');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[4] masked');
+{
+  const filter = { active: () => true, mask: async (s) => String(s).split(CARD).join('[PII:CARD#0badf00d]'), unmaskDeep: async (a) => a };
+  const [r] = await run(dir, [`const t = await read("card.txt"); console.log("line has " + t); return "ok"`], { filter });
+  ok(/\[PII:CARD#0badf00d\]/.test(r.content) && !r.content.includes(CARD), 'a token in the record, never the digits');
+  const lg = await logText(dir, r);
+  ok(lg && /\[PII:CARD#0badf00d\]/.test(lg) && !lg.includes(CARD) && !lg.includes(CARD.replace(/ /g, '')), 'and the same in the log');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[5] parallel()');
+{
+  const [r] = await run(dir, ['const x = await parallel({ good: async () => { console.log("good ran"); return await read("a.txt"); }, bad: async () => { await read("missing.txt"); } }); return x']);
+  const rec = recordOf(r);
+  ok(r.status === 'error' && /parallel branch failed \(the others finished\) - "bad"/.test(r.content), `the failure names the branch (${String(r.content).split('\n')[0].slice(0, 90)})`);
+  ok(/── parallel branch "good" ──\nconsole: good ran\n\[\d\] read\(a\.txt\) · \d+ms → hello\n── end of "good" ──/.test(rec), 'the other branch finished, in its own section, with its console line and result');
+  ok(/── parallel branch "bad" ──\n\[\d\] read\(missing\.txt\)/.test(rec), 'the failing branch has its own section');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[6] non-zero exits');
+{
+  const [r] = await run(dir, ['await node(["fail.js"]); return "carried on"']);
+  ok(r.status === 'error' && /node fail\.js exited with code 4/.test(r.content) && /the real reason/.test(r.content) && !/carried on/.test(r.content), 'a non-zero exit stops the snippet, with the end of the output');
+  const [r2] = await run(dir, ['const x = await node(["fail.js"], { allowFail: true }); return "code " + x.code']);
+  ok(r2.status !== 'error' && /^code 4/.test(r2.content), '{allowFail: true} returns it as data');
+  ok(/exit 4\nstderr:\nthe real reason\nstdout:\npartial work/.test(recordOf(r2)), 'and the record shows the exit code and the output');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[7] the agent can read its own log');
+{
+  const [first] = await run(dir, ['const b = await read("big.txt"); return "len " + b.length']);
+  const [r] = await run(dir, [`const t = await read(${JSON.stringify(first.logPath)}); return t.includes("END_OF_BIG") ? "FOUND" : "missing"`]);
+  ok(/^FOUND/.test(r.content), `read() of the log works (${String(r.content).slice(0, 40)})`);
+}
+
+console.log('\n[8] the UI shows the complete record for a finished snippet');
+{

+  if (!chrome) console.log('  (skipped — Chrome not found)');

+    const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8')).replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG={"defaultModel":"m","baseURL":"(mock)"};</script></head>');
